Chroma ATE (2360.TW)
06 October 2026 Citi Research
3
Chroma ATE
Valuation
Our target price of NT$2,500 is based on 40x 2027E P/E, at close to 1x PEG. Chroma shares have re-rated above its historical
range as its growth outlook is now strongly tied to AI infrastructure build out. We estimate 60%+ of its revenue is now AI-
related, with strong growth across product segments including power testers, SLT, metrology, and photonics. Chroma has a
commanding market share in most of these segments and should be well positioned to capitalize on the global AI expansion.

Risks
Downside risks to achieving our target price include: 1) softer AI/CPO growth expectation due to changes in capex outlook or
production bottleneck; 2) fewer SLT needed as a result of shortened cycle time as GPU yield rate improves; 3) disappointing
metrology shipment due to changes at ramp-up schedule; 4) testing process change leading to loss in market share; 5) FX risks
(50-60% of Chroma’s sales are in US$, 10-20% in Rmb).
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
We introduce 2027 forecast at 90.4K racks (+10% y/y) vs our 2026 forecast now
at 82K racks: This year is a strong one for downstream rack assemblers, with rack

shipments up ~209% y/y, but we expect rack shipments to grow another ~10% y/y
to reach 90.4K in CY27. For CY27, our forecast implies Hon Hai will be a share gainer
with rack share up to 43% (from 37% in CY26), with Quanta staying unchanged at
26% and Wistron's share up slightly to 24% (from 23% in CY26).
Quanta (2382.TW, OW): September revenue was NT$325.3B (-23% m/m, +77% y/y).
We attribute the m/m decline mainly to lower GB200/300 rack shipments at 1.55-

1.60K racks (-38% m/m), despite being offset slightly by higher NB shipments at
4.2M units (+62% m/m, -9% y/y). In 3Q, Quanta shipped ~6.2K racks (+3% q/q),
which came in slightly lower than our forecast of ~6.4K racks. Total 3Q revenue
reached ~NT$1,116M (+8% q/q, +125% y/y), which is 15% above MSe but in-line with
the Street. For 2026, we forecast Quanta to ship 20.9K racks, and we introduce our
preliminary 2027 forecast at 23.5K (+12% y/y).
Wistron (3231.TW, OW): September revenue was  NT$380.8B (-17% m/m, +87% y/

y). We attribute the m/m decline to: 1) lower GB200/300 server computing tray
shipments  (1.25-1.30K rack equivalents, by our estimates, down approximately 46%
m/m), despite higher Wiwynn revenue at ~NT$172.6B (+20% m/m, +100% y/y) and
higher NB shipments at 1.5M units (+7% m/m, -32% y/y), flat DT shipments at 500K
(flat m/m, -44% y/y), and higher monitor shipments at 950K (+27% m/m, +6% y/y).
In 3Q, Wistron shipped ~4.8K racks (+22% q/q), which came in slightly lower than

our forecast of ~5.1K racks. Total 3Q revenue reached ~NT$1,149M (+28% q/q,
+102% y/y), which is 10% above MSe and 9% above the Street. For 2026, we
forecast Wistron to ship 19.13K racks, and we introduce our preliminary 2027
forecast at 22K (+15% y/y).
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of
interest that could affect the objectivity of Morgan Stanley

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
We estimate GB200/300 rack output in September  at ~6.5K (-23% m/m).
Quanta shipped 1.55-1.60K  GB200/GB300 racks.
Wistron shipped 1.25-1.30K GB200/GB300 racks.
Hon Hai shipped ~2.7K GB200/300 racks.
We also introduce our 2027 forecast at 90.4K racks (+10% y/y).
October 12, 2026 01:46 PM GMT

Hon Hai (2317.TW, OW): September revenue was NT$1,158.6B (+26% m/m, +38% y/
y), with consumer segment revenue seeing the strongest m/m growth, followed by
components and other products, cloud and networking products, and lastly
computing. We model HH's GB200/300 rack shipments at 2.7K (+4% m/m). This
brings total 3Q rack shipments at 7.8K racks (+1% q/q). For 2026, we forecast
Foxconn to ship 30.3K racks, and we introduce our preliminary 2027 forecast at

38.8K (+28% y/y).

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Hon Hai Precision (2317.TW)
Base case scenario value, derived from a residual income valuation model. Our key assump-
tions include a cost of equity of 8.5%, a medium-term growth rate of 13%, and a terminal
growth rate of 3%.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected iPhone sell-through
n Faster progress in AI server business
n Faster EV business development progress
n Any new M&A activity that could improve sentiment

Risks to Downside
n Lower iPhone sell-through
n Smaller profit contribution from AI business
n Slower EV business development progress
n Geopolitical developments that could negatively affect foreign investment
Quanta Computer Inc. (2382.TW)
Base case, residual income model. Key assumptions include a cost of equity of 9.0% (beta of
1.2, equity premium of 6.0% and risk-free rate of 1.5%), an 8.5% medium-term growth rate, and
a 3% terminal growth rate.

Risks to Upside
n Stronger-than-expected NB demand
n Stronger-than-expected Apple Watch demand
n Stronger-than-expected server demand
n Faster-than-expected AI server penetration
Risks to Downside
n Weaker-than-expected NB demand
n Softer-than-expected Apple Watch demand
n Weak margin performance owing to rising labor costs and sales shortfalls
n Fierce price competition in the mega data center segment
n Slower-than-expected AI server penetration

Wistron Corporation (3231.TW)
Base case, residual income valuation. Key assumptions: 8.7% cost of equity, 7.0% medium-
term growth rate and 3% terminal growth rate.
Risks to Upside
n Faster-than-expected divestiture of consumer electronics business
n Stronger-than-expected NB demand
n Margin expansion from better product mix

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

研 究  報 告                
晶呈科技(4768.TT) 
產業：化學工業 
氣體原料量產降本效應與玻璃加工產品放量，推動明年獲利高成⻑ 
 投資評價與建議 
考量三廠C4F6⼀級合成放量顯著優化⽑利率重回30%，且AI驅動TGV及CPO
加工產品步入規模化量產，⻑期動能強勁，參考台股半導體特化與先進封裝概
念股本益比30-35倍，因短期受高額研發費用投入、新廠折舊與轉虧為盈初期
致使當前本益比失真，故採用TGV產線滿載Forward PER。 
 重點評論分析 
特氣出貨回升，TGV量產放量：推估4Q26隨特氣出貨回升及客⼾去庫存告⼀
段落，營收可望季增73.7%至6.6億元，全年營收估增50.9%至18.6億元，玻璃
加工受惠AI高階封裝與CPO需求，GCB及One Piece Glass FAU已進入規模量
產，預估2H26營收較上半年成⻑230%，惟三廠延至4Q26試量產，特氣原料
成本改善遞延，⽑利率下修至23%，加上研發費用增加，使稅後EPS 0.29元。  
⼀級合成與高⽑利產品帶動獲利躍升： 預估2027年營收年增35.1%至25.1億
元，受惠AI ASIC大廠特氣需求放量、TGV及矽光子共同封裝相關應用擴產6倍
產能，並拓展生醫檢測市場，預計3Q27末相關產線陸續量產，Stripper隨認證
客⼾增加至9家，⽑利率因三廠C4F6⼀級合成與純化工廠預計1Q27放量，年
產能250噸，可降低外購原料成本，加上Stripper產能利用率提升，以及TGV、
生醫檢測及Stripper等高⽑利產品營收占比提升至20%，帶動年增7.92ppts至
30.9% ， 營益率升至12.5% ， 稅前盈餘年增936%至2.91億元 ， 稅後EPS 4.30元 。  
 投資風險 
⼀級合成新廠量產進度與高⽑利產品TGV 、 特化投產或客⼾導入進度不如預期 。

### Page 2

2 
| 公司簡介  
公司所做的玻璃通孔技術(Through Glass Via ,TGV)主要應用於大積體電路玻璃載板(Glass Core Su bstrate, 
GCS)(非 Glass interposer)，是做為⼀個玻璃核心晶圓加工廠(TGV Glass Foundry)，並將加工好 的玻璃核心層
(Glass Core Board, GCB)，交付給下游 IC 載板廠或封測廠後，在玻璃核心的外側壓合 ABF 絕緣膜，並製作精細
的銅線路 ， 組合成完整的 GCS ． 亦透過玻璃加工技術開發⼀體成型式 CPO 光纖束導基座(One Piece Glass FAU)。 
GCB 是數個晶片叢共用的超大玻璃地基，支撐所有晶片和並保持封裝的平坦和穩定，解決穩定性(平坦度與結構支
撐)的瓶頸，位置在整個封裝體的最底層，玻璃材料解決熱膨脹、曲蹺度、以及對封裝尺寸的要求極限，目前公司
的製作玻璃微孔的貫穿深度與孔道寬度(孔徑)的比值已達到深寬比 100:1，全孔立體型態增圓度可達 99%、板厚
可達 1.85mm，單位面積產值相較 Glass Interposer 多 25-30 倍，在硬脆且易碎的玻璃材質上實現百倍深寬比，
是公司透過獨家氣態分解融蝕技術，在微觀垂直孔道上的極致控制力。 
One Piece Glass FAU 是⼀種應用於 CPO(共同封裝光學)與超高速光通訊系統中光電整合模組，超微細光纖束的
精準對位組裝，專為插裝與固定直徑僅 125 微米(µm)的超微細光纖束所設計(直徑極細，大約三根光纖才等於⼀
根頭髮的粗細)，在 AI 晶片架構從傳統電訊號傳輸轉向光訊號傳輸的過程中，該產品主要解決(1)微米級光纖對位
極難與光訊號損耗問題，公司雷射改質與氣態蝕刻技術(LADY)，在玻璃上打造出高⼀致性的微孔結構，提供高精
度的光纖對位，顯著提高光耦合效率(Optical Coupling Efficiency)，可減少多件式組裝產生的累積公差(2)高溫運
轉下的熱膨脹與光軸偏斜，利用玻璃材料極低熱膨脹係數的物理特性，確保光纖對位結構在⻑期高溫運算下仍保
持極佳的尺寸，(3)光軸穩定性以及光電整合元件的結構精密性，採用⼀體成型(One-Piece)的玻璃基座設計，結合
TGV 製程的高垂直度與高精度特性，配合客⼾在 AI 系統架構升級中對光電整合元件的規模化部署需求。 
圖表、Glass Core Board (左圖)不同象限與One Piece Glass FAU(右圖)顯微鏡下示意圖

資料來源：玉山投顧、公司資料 
| 營  運  概  況  
外銷與特氣出貨拉動累計營業收入倍增，受費用提升致⽑利率承壓：1H26 合併營收 8.21 億元，YoY+141%，主
要受惠特殊氣體外銷出口的大幅增加，氣體出貨支數達 13,022 支，其中 2Q26 營收 QoQ-13.9%至 3.8 億元，主
因在於部分外銷出口出貨量有所減少至 6,294 支，受韓國等記憶體客⼾要求調整出貨影響，但⽑利率
QoQ+11.1ppts 至 24.4%，因低⽑利產品(海外記憶體客⼾的特氣)相對 1Q26 出貨較少，1H26 ⽑利率 YoY-
20.45ppts 至 18.5%，除了產品組合關係，另因上游⼀級原料合成⾃給率尚未開出，使得原料成本負擔較沉重，
2Q26 費用率從 17.6%提升至 25.6%，主要來⾃研究發展費用大幅提升從 7.1%提升到 12.2%，目的在於為竹北五
廠、頭份三廠等新廠建置進行關鍵技術研發，1H26 稅前虧損 0.36 億元，稅後 EPS -0.99 元。 
特氣與 TGV 放量帶動 2H26 營收優於 1H26：3Q26 單季營收 3.8 億元，ＱoQ-0.6%，推估 3Q26 鋼瓶出貨量
6,000-6,200 支，主要來⾃ C4F6 內外銷出貨持續，⽑利率維持 24%，推估 4Q26 營收 QoQ+73.7%至 6.6 億元， 
4Q26 鋼瓶出貨量 7,500-7,800 支，2H26 鋼瓶出貨合計約 13,500-14,000 支，比 1H26 出貨量成⻑約 7.7%，隨
著全球半導體大廠的新產能與新廠陸續開出，帶動對氣體 C4F6 蝕刻氣體、Laser Gas 等的剛性需求持續增加，另

外 2H26 隨客⼾去庫存化使需求回補，帶動氣體鋼瓶出貨量提升，並受惠於 AI 伺服器與高階封裝和矽光子共同封
裝(Co-Packaged Optics, CPO)，使玻璃加工市場快速成⻑，帶動相關特殊氣體的需求同步提升。 
玻璃加工技術在 TGV Glass Foundry 領域應用，已成功建置兩大可量產的核心產品線，配合年底 2 條產線(月產
能約 1 萬片)到位與量產放量，預估 2H26 營收相較 1H26 成⻑ 230%，主因為 GCB 與 One Piece Glass FAU 皆
已跨越工程驗證，正式進入單月接單與出貨片數達四位數以上的規模量產的放量階段，預估單片 150x150mm(5
萬孔/片)價值約$500-700 美元。 
特化產品 Stripper 目前設有⼀條年產能 288 頓產線，目前已被客⼾認證達 15 個品項，其中 7 個已達穩定交貨，
驗證通過的客⼾數已增加至９家，預估 2H26 營收相較 1H26 成⻑ 3%，加計其他產品，預估 2026 全年營收成⻑
率下修至 50.9%達 18.5 億元 ， 由於三廠(C4F6 ⼀級合成工廠)投產時程 ， 從先前 3Q26 延後至 4Q26 進行試量產 ，
使全年⽑利率從原先預估的 35%下修至 23%，加上研發費用擴張，下修稅前盈餘年成⻑ 41.3%至 0.28 億元，稅
後E P S  0 . 2 9元 。  
⼀級合成放量大幅優化特氣成本結構，TGV 導入 AI 大廠放量，帶動獲利大幅成⻑：邁入 2027 年，預估全年營收
成⻑ 35.1%至 25.1 億元，主要成⻑來⾃特殊氣體核心業務持續受惠 AI ASIC 大廠需求放量，另外玻璃加工應用於
AI 先進封裝與矽光子共同封裝產品預計於 3Q27 末累計建置達 12 條產線並正式量產，推動營收規模呈指數級成
⻑，在生醫檢測應用(竹北五廠)的最新進程上，已於生醫園區簽約購入竹北五廠房，將玻璃加工技術應用於生產生
醫用檢測儀器，在玻璃培養⽫微雕出高⼀致性的 3D 微孔陣列，模擬人體內真實的立體腫瘤微環境(3D 全立體型
態載具(Booster))，針對 EGFR 基因突變陽性的局部晚期非小細胞肺癌細胞進行精準控制併用標靶藥物泰格沙
(Tagrisso®)之細胞檢測實驗體外評估(In Vitro) ， 提升藥物對癌細胞的抑制與殺傷成效 ， 並藉由開發的生醫檢測儀
器，作為癌症精準用藥的關鍵數據，預計五廠將於 3Q27 末正式開始量產，此應用將使玻璃加工技術跨足高⽑利
生醫市場，現有廠房空間至少可規劃 6 條產線，後續將視客⼾需求進行滾動擴充。Stripper 隨 2026 年認證客⼾
增加朝向產能滿載，以及二廠無水氟化氫(AHF)量產線與三氯化硼(BCl3)試產線裝機完成，四條產線年產值上看 3-
4 億元的效益下，穩定貢獻營收。2027 全年⽑利率則受惠頭份三廠的 C4F6 ⼀級合成與純化工廠預計於 1Q27 正
式放量，設計年產能 250 噸，特氣⾃給率將大幅拉升，改變過去依賴外購二、三級純化原料的成本結構，使成本
大幅降低，加上特用化學品業務在產能利用率拉升，高⽑利產品如 TGV 玻璃載板、生醫檢測儀器、Stripper ⾃製
放量的佔比合計提升至 20%，使⽑利率顯著提升 7.92ppts 至 30.9%，費用率隨營業收入規模擴大而稀釋至 18.36%，
營益率提升 9.75ppt 至 12.5%，稅前盈餘年成⻑ 936%至 2.91 億元，稅後 EPS 4.30 元。

ab 8 October 2026
Global Research
Initiation of Coverage
WinWay Technology
Final point of contact: securing AI yield through
advanced testing socket
Solid growth ahead with robust capacity expansions
We initiate coverage of AI/HPC test socket leader WinWay with a Buy rating and NT
$8,900 price target. WinWay's share price has corrected 43% from its April 2026 peak
due to broader market de-rating and margin pressure associated with its Technoprobe

partnership and capacity expansion. We believe the recent correction presents an
opportunity to re-engage with WinWay's long-term growth trajectory, supported by
three key drivers: 1) increasing content from AI GPUs, CPUs, and TPUs, 2) substantial
capacity expansion, and 3) broader adoption of system-level testing (SLT). Together,
these drivers should support revenue growth of 105%/74%/71% in 2026E/27E/28E.
Importantly, WinWay's growth is increasingly being driven by higher socket content per

device rather than semiconductor unit growth alone and the stock currently trades at
16x of our 2028E P/E vs historical average of 30x, potential to re-rate with GM recovery
in the coming quarters. See our companion testing deep dive sector note.
Rising yields and utilisation to offset probe card dilution
Gross margin declined to 38.3% in Q226 from 45.3% in FY25, pressured by outsourced
MEMS probes and expansion-related costs. While probe-card margins are unlikely to

return to H125 levels of 48.9% given the Technoprobe partnership structure, we expect
margin pressure to ease as utilization and yields improve, supporting GM recovery to
43.1% in 2027E and 44.7% in 2028E.
CPU and TPU as fundamental growth drivers
Beyond its core GPU socket franchise,  WinWay is well positioned to benefit from the
expansion of AI-oriented CPU and TPU platforms. We expect the company to supply
sockets for AMD's Venice CPU, Nvidia's Vera CPU, and Google's TPU v8t and v9, and

also Vera CPU probe cards, providing an additional growth engine beyond GPUs.
WinWay is well positioned in this segment due to its historical collaboration with end
customers and its proximity to the local Taiwanese test supply chain. We view AI CPUs
and TPUs as an increasingly important contributor to test interface demand as compute
architectures diversify in the agentic AI era, driving WinWay's socket and probe card
sales growth of 132%/75%/69% and 94%/72%/78% in 2026E/27E/28E, respectively.

We estimate CPU/TPU to contribute  around 30%/20% of WinWay's 2027E sales.
Valuation: Initiate with a Buy rating, with price target of NT$8,900
We forecast EPS of NT$101.8 in 2026E, rising to NT$201.4/393.0 in 2027E/28E. Our NT
$8,900 price target is based on 30x average 2027E-28E P/E, in line with the company's
historical average. We believe this valuation is supported by WinWay's exposure to
structural growth in AI/HPC testing, expanding socket content, and our long-term

earnings CAGR estimate of 30%. We initiate with a Buy rating.

WinWay Technology 8 October 2026 ab 2
WinWay Technology UBS Research
Thesis Map     UBS                    Research     THESIS MAP a guide to our thinking and what´s where in this report
Q: Will capacity constraints limit WinWay's growth in the next two to three years?
Near term, yes. Beyond 2026E, no. WinWay's current capacity remains constrained, but ongoing
expansions should significantly increase output over the next two years. With new capacity coming

online and the company shifting toward higher-value AI/HPC applications, including CPU probe
cards, >10k-pin coaxial sockets and next-generation HyperSockets, we forecast revenue growth of
105%/74%/71% in 2026E/27E/28E, supported by capacity additions, rising AI/HPC content and
expanding socket demand. We also expect operating leverage to improve as capacity scales and
utilization increases, with OPM rising from 27.5% in 2026E to 32.1%/36.7% in 2027E/28E.

Q: Will WinWay's GM recover from Q226's 38.3%?
Yes. We view recent margin pressure as largely temporary. GM has been impacted by MEMS probe
sourcing through Technoprobe and start-up costs associated with capacity expansion. Although
margin dilution from probe card business is unlikely to unravel due to the MEMS partnership terms
with Technoprobe, as new facilities ramp and yields improve, we expect profitability to recover
gradually from the Q226 trough, with gross margin improving to 43.1% in 2027E and 44.7% in

2028E.
We initiate coverage of WinWay with a Buy rating and NT$8,900 price target. We believe the market
is overly focused on near-term margin pressure and underappreciating WinWay's long-term growth
outlook. Key drivers include 1) expanding content from AI GPUs, CPUs, and TPUs, 2) substantial
capacity expansion, and 3) growing adoption of system-level testing (SLT). Together, these drivers
should support revenue growth of 105%/74%/71% in 2026E/27E/28E.

TSMC and OSATs have been expanding their CoWoS capacities aggressively, from 40kwpm in 2024
to around 90 kwpm in 2025, and is tracking to expand CoWoS capacity toward 160kwpm  in 2026E.
TSMC also ramped up expanding N3/N2 capacity, with multiple product launches from customers'
pipelines—Nvidia's Rubin, AMD's MI400 series/Venice CPU and other cloud ASICs—translating into
foreseeable, strong testing demand as the majority are WinWay's customers. AI accelerator test times

have increased meaningfully for new platforms. We expect continued front-end and advanced
packaging investments to drive higher testing intensity and support long-term demand for advanced
sockets and test interfaces.
WinWay's share price has corrected 43% from its April 2026 peak and currently trades at 16x of our
2028E P/E vs historical average of 30x. Our reverse DCF analysis indicates that the current market
price implies only a 19% long-term sales CAGR vs our estimate of 25%, alongside long-term GM

level of 32% vs our estimate of 45%. We see scope for re-rating.

WinWay was founded in 2001 in Kaoshiung, Taiwan. It is a semiconductor testing interface solutions
provider, with final test socket as  its main business segment. The company has high-end solutions for
AI/HPC testing, providing final test/system-level test and burn-in test sockets and probe cards through
its own made probes and partnership with Technoprobe.

### Page 3

WinWay Technology 8 October 2026 ab 3
WinWay Technology UBS Research
Testing TAM expanding with IC complexity: leaders likely to
outperform industry growth
Our central view is that testing content per AI accelerator is growing faster than
semiconductor unit volumes, creating a multi-year expansion in testing TAM. We believe
semiconductor testing is entering a period of structural growth as AI-driven chip
complexity increases testing intensity across wafer, package and system levels. The

industry's shift-left strategy, which moves more testing upstream in the semiconductor
manufacturing process, is resulting in longer and more frequent test insertions. As cloud
AI GPUs and ASICs become increasingly complex, additional testing is required to ensure
functionality, performance and yield, while reducing defective parts per million (DPPM).
More robust testing should improve deployment efficiency and lower the cost of
operating AI infrastructure.

Semiconductor testing generally comprises four stages: wafer/chip probing (CP), final
test (FT), system-level test (SLT), and burn-in. CP is conducted at the wafer-level before
packaging, while FT, SLT, and burn-in occur after packaging. The below Figure 1S e m i c o n d u c t o r  t e s t i n g  p r o c e s s :  f r o m  w a f e r  l e v e l  t o  p a c k a g e  l e v e l
illustrates the semiconductor manufacturing flow.
Figure 1: Semiconductor testing process: from wafer level to package level

We estimate semiconductor testing historically accounts for ~1% of total
semiconductor revenue. Within testing, consumables such as probe cards, sockets and
interface boards represent ~10-15% of industry revenue. According to Yole, the probe
card, socket and interface board markets should grow from US$3.8bn, US$2.3bn and
US$1.7bn in 2026 to US$5.5bn, US$3.1bn and US$2.0bn by 2031, respectively.

Nonetheless, leaders within the testing sector will standout among competition, with
greater sales CAGR comparing to the industry.
As the leader of AI/HPC sockets, WinWay is set to deliver 2026-31E socket sales CAGR
of 33%, underpinned by robust demand for final test and system-level test, with test

content and test times rising which each iteration of new AI accelerators/CPU, more
sockets and equipment will be needed in order to increase the throughput.

WinWay is expanding capacity rapidly to capture this growing TAM. Industry test
interface suppliers are expanding capacity aggressively to meet rising AI and HPC
demand. As advanced semiconductors require higher-performance probe cards, sockets
and interface boards, capacity additions are becoming increasingly important to support

growth across both wafer-level and package-level testing.
We believe WinWay is well positioned to gain share within the high-end AI/HPC test
interface market. Taiwanese suppliers benefit from their proximity to leading fabless
customers and the growing preference for localised supply chains, particularly for
advanced testing applications where responsiveness and engineering support are
critical. Against a backdrop of tight industry supply, WinWay announced significant

capacity expansion plans over the next two to three years.
WinWay's capacity is expected by the company to increase from 3.5mn pins per month
at year-end 2025 to 6mn currently, reaching 9mn by year-end 2026 and 14mn by year-
end 2027 through expansions in Renwu, Kaohsiung. By 2027, approximately 60% of
pogo-pin requirements should be internally sourced, strengthening both supply security
and margins.
We estimate these capacity additions will support revenue growth of 105%/74%/71%

in 2026E/27E/28E.

The AI era has materially increased the value of semiconductor testing. Compared with
traditional mobile and automotive chips, AI accelerators incorporate larger compute
dies, more advanced nodes, chiplet architectures, advanced packaging technologies,
multiple I/O dies, and increasing HBM content. These factors drive higher-value test
consumables, including advanced MEMS probe cards, multilayer interface boards, and

next-generation sockets designed to support higher pin counts, thermal loading, and
power delivery requirements.
Capacity expansion by test interface vendors is ultimately supported by continued
investment throughout the advanced packaging ecosystem, from providers such as
TSMC, ASE, Intel, and Amkor. During the Q226 earnings calls, TSMC raised its recent
2026 capex from US$52-56bn to US$60-64bn and ASE raised its 2026 capex from US
$8.5bn to US$10.5bn, with both companies citing robust demand supporting capex

raise, we believe capex for the next two years will be even higher than 2026, indicating
continuous growth in future years. With advanced packaging technologies getting
significantly more complex and with larger form factor, illustrated by the upcoming
ramp of both TSMC's CoPoS (chip-on-panel-on-substrate) and Intel's EMIB-T
(embedded multi-die interconnect bridge) packaging technologies, test interface
solution providers will benefit with more value added consumables (see our CoPoS vs

EMIB-T deep dive).

New iterations of semiconductors, especially for AI / HPC applications, are set to increase
in both front-end wafer fabrication advancements and back-end packaging
complexities with each iterations (i.e. Nvidia's roadmap from Blackwell to Rubin to

Feynman or Google's TPU roadmap from v7 to v8 to v9).   This trend is evident with
TSMC's front-end  and TSMC, Intel, and OSAT's back-end advanced packaging capacity
expansions. For front-end, we see industry N3 capacity expanding from 156kwpm in
year end 2025 to 224/283/293kpwm in year end 2026E/27E/28E, and N2 capacity from
47kwpm in year-end 2025 to 111/200/312kpwm in year end 2026E/27E/28E. For back-
end, we are seeing industry CoWoS capacity to rise from 90kwpm in 2025 to 160kwpm

in 2026E and further expand to 270kwpm in 2027E.

Final-test, system-level test, and burn-in content should continue to increase as AI

accelerators become larger, more power-hungry and more complex. Rising thermal
design power and packaging complexity increase validation requirements, driving

### Page 7

WinWay Technology 8 October 2026 ab 7
longer test times and higher demand for test equipment to prevent testing from
becoming a production bottleneck. This is where WinWay's sockets comes into play—
as high-end chips with 2.5D/3D/3.5D packaging requires intricate FT sockets to handle
the high thermal design power (TDP) and high data rates of up to 224G during the FT
process for reliable test results.

While architectural changes can affect test requirements for individual product
generations, we expect the long-term trend of rising testing content to remain intact.
For example, Nvidia's Rubin Ultra adopts a simpler package structure than previously
expected, from a 4-die structure to a 2-die structure and Rubin Ultra HBM de-spec from
HBM4E 12-Hi to initially use HBM4 8-Hi and upgrade to HBM4E 8-Hi at the latter stages,

which may moderate near-term test growth. However, future products such as Nvidia's
Feynman are expected to reintroduce greater package complexity, with a four-GPU per
package structure. More importantly, any reduction in test time can be offset by higher
production volumes, making throughput requirements equally important for testing
demand.
We expect test interface content growth to outpace unit growth, For instance, test
socket's ASP directly correlates with its pin counts, with the addition of 20-30% more

pins inside the sockets, socket ASP can grow 20-30% or more as the socket might
require significant cooling capabilities as the TDP of the DUT rises with its larger form
factor. Unlike prior semiconductor cycles, much of the current demand is driven by rising
test interface content per device rather than unit growth alone.
Robust growth drivers across semiconductor testing
We believe WinWay is set to benefit from several semiconductor testing growth drivers

and capture the AI induced, expanding testing TAM. We see three main growth drivers
for WinWay: 1) AI accelerators (GPU + TPU), 2) agentic AI driven CPU demand, and 3)
shift-left testing trend. Below we list the three main growth drivers in detail.
Accelerators still in focus with TPU as another significant growth driver
Nvidia has been the primary driver of testing content growth over the past several years,
benefiting the entire semiconductor testing supply chain. We expect this trend to

continue as GPU architectures evolve from Blackwell to Rubin and then to Feynman,
with increasing process node complexity, larger package sizes and more advanced
packaging technologies driving higher testing value per device.
We believe Nvidia should remain a major contributor to  WinWay, with GPUs continuing
to serve as their core growth engine. We estimate WinWay's socket share could improve
from a roughly 50/50 split with Smiths Interconnect in prior generations to 60/40 with

Rubin. Nvidia currently accounts for approximately 50% of WinWay's revenue. Both
Nvidia GPUs and Google TPUs increase testing demand through higher content per
device rather than unit growth alone.

### Page 8

WinWay Technology 8 October 2026 ab 8
Beyond Nvidia, Google's TPU roadmap represents a significant incremental growth
opportunity.  In our view, WinWay should benefit as the socket supplier for both TPU v8t
and the next-generation TPU v9 platform. We believe MediaTek's increasing share gain
for Google TPUs will also benefit the local Taiwanese test interface supply chain. We
estimate TPU to contribute to around 20% of WinWay 2027E sales.
We view TPU v9 as a particularly attractive opportunity given its large four-die package

architecture based on Intel's EMIB-T packaging technology, which should drive higher
testing content. We estimate production of 4mn TPU v8t units in 2027E, followed by
2.3mn TPU v9 packages (9.2mn dies) and an additional 2mn TPU v8t units in 2028E.
Importantly, while EMIB-T packaging is expected to be performed in Malaysia, we
believe the majority of advanced testing value should remain within the Taiwanese
testing ecosystem. Leading testing providers, including ASE and KYEC, maintain

substantial testing operations across Taiwan and Southeast Asia, supporting continued
participation by Taiwanese test interface suppliers. See our latest MediaTek report and
EMIB-T deep dive.
CPU business as a supplemental catalyst for test interface companies in
the agentic world
We believe semiconductor test interface are an underappreciated beneficiary of the
emerging agentic AI ecosystem. UBS estimates the CPU market will expand to US
$170bn by 2030E from US$31bn in 2025 and US$71bn in 2027E, driven by rising

deployment of AI infrastructure. As CPU-to-GPU ratios increasingly approach 1:1 in AI
clusters, demand for testing interfaces should expand alongside CPU volumes. TSMC is
the main CP test provider in the industry, with probe card suppliers aiding its massive
demand by supplying probes, such as TSMC's GPU probe cards supported by
Technoprobe's probes. However, with capacity now becoming scarce with strong front
and back-end demand, TSMC is outsourcing its CP business to ASE and KYEC, further

broadening the testing supply chain. This is where new probe card vendors come in—
smaller probe card players in the past, such as  WinWay, now has the opportunity to gain
significant, non-GPU share from TSMC, as they have MEMS probe capabilities
(WinWay's MEMS probe are from its strategic partnership with Technoprobe) and has a
long established relationship with the fabless companies such as Nvidia via supplying
non-probe card consumables such as sockets. Of which, CPU projects has become the

main area for  WinWay to expand their footprint.
We expect WinWay to benefit from the ramp of Nvidia's ARM-based Vera CPU and
Google's ARM-based Axion CPU in 2026E-27E. WinWay is positioned to supply sockets
and probe cards for Vera and sockets for Axion. Additional upside could come from
AMD's Venice CPU, where WinWay is well positioned. For WinWay, CPU-related
demand could account for approximately 30% of sales in 2026E-27E. Importantly, CPU
programmes diversify exposure beyond AI GPUs and provide a second structural growth

driver for test interface suppliers.
Shift-left igniting secular growth for chip probing
The industry's adoption of shift-left testing should drive a multi-year structural growth
opportunity for chip probing (CP) and its supply chain. As advanced packaging costs rise
and AI accelerators become increasingly complex, semiconductor manufacturers are
placing greater emphasis on identifying known-good dies (KGD) before packaging. The
test shift-left trend will  drive structural AI/HPC probe card and test consumables

demand, with increasing test interface content as test insertions and test time increase.
WinWay should also benefit through its partnership with Technoprobe, with probe card
revenue growth remaining robust at 94%/72% in 2026E/27E following 161%/260%
growth in 2024/25.

The economics of advanced packaging reinforce this trend. Heterogeneous integration,
chiplet architectures and 2.5D/3D packaging significantly increase package value, while

defects identified late in the manufacturing process can lead to substantial scrap costs.
To mitigate this risk, foundries and OSATs such as TSMC and ASE employ distributed
testing flows incorporating KGD, known-good-stack (KGS) and assembly test screening,
to catch defects before the components enter a multi-week packaging cycle (>2 weeks
for CoW and 2 weeks for oS). The importance of these tests rises as AI clusters become
increasingly dependent on component reliability and low defective-parts-per-million

(DPPM) rates. Given the multiplicative yield degradation, a 300bps yield decline at just
one manufacturing stage (e.g. dropping from 99.5% to 96.5%) reduces overall
integrated four-stage yield from 98.0% to 95.0%; on a 500,000 unit ramp at US
$10,000 per unit, this 3% scrap delta translates into around US$150m in unrecoverable
losses given >6-month manufacturing lead times.
At the same time, rising chip power consumption is increasing the technical difficulty of

wafer-level testing. As chip thermal design power scales beyond 1,000W and power
density is expected by the industry to surge  another 8x by 2030, traditional wafer-level
chip probe (WLCP) encounters severe physical bottlenecks. Finer probe pitches and
higher power densities increase the risk of overheating, probe needle burnout and yield
degradation, creating demand for more advanced probing and thermal-control
solutions.

WinWay  was founded in 2001 in Kaohsiung, Taiwan, and is a leading provider of
semiconductor testing interface solutions, with test sockets representing its core
business. The company focuses on advanced AI/HPC applications, supplying sockets for

final test (FT), system-level test (SLT) and burn-in, as well as cobra probe cards  and
partnership with Technoprobe for MEMS probe cards.
WinWay's business is primarily divided into two segments:
 Test socket: Sockets are WinWay's largest revenue contributor. The company
supplies a broad range of testing sockets, including coaxial, PoP/plastic and burn-
in sockets, serving applications ranging from consumer electronics to high-
performance computing.

 Coaxial socket: utilised as the mainstream option for nearly all AI/HPC chips,
as traditional sockets are near its physical limits. Coaxial sockets need to handle
data rates of up to 224G for high speed transmission and high chip TDP during
the FT process.
 HyperSocket: a rubber-silicone like layer called elastomer are placed on top
of the pogo pins, these elastomer pins causes zero damage to the chips being
tested under high contact forces. WinWay's next generation HyperSocket

technology utilises elastomers to provide a softer contact between the DUT
and the pogo pins, avoiding damage to solder ball. This structure significantly
enhances spring probe's lifetime, reduces cleaning frequency, and achieves
maximum production efficiency. We estimate it will contribute around 5% of
sales in 2027E with a 20-30% ASP premium as compared to standard coaxial
socket.
 Probe cards:  WinWay historically supplies cobra probe cards (vertical probe

cards), but with the company announcing their five-year probe supply deal with
Technoprobe for MEMS probe in December 2025, they are now one of Nvida Vera
CPU's main probe card suppliers.
AI accelerators are pushing socket requirements beyond 10,000 pins, making coaxial
sockets the industry standard for leading-edge AI/HPC devices. These sockets must
support transmission speeds of up to 224G while handling significantly higher thermal
design power (TDP) during testing.

Elastomer technology is increasingly being adopted alongside spring-probe (pogo-pin)
architectures to reduce chip damage and improve contact reliability. WinWay's next-
generation HyperSocket integrates elastomer materials between the DUT and spring
probes, improving probe lifetime, reducing cleaning requirements and enhancing
production efficiency. We believe HyperSocket could become an important
differentiator as next-generation AI accelerators demand higher pin counts, bandwidth

and thermal performance. We estimate HyperSocket could contribute approximately
5% of WinWay's revenue in 2027E while carrying a 20-30% ASP premium versus
conventional coaxial sockets. The transition toward larger AI packages, higher pin
counts and faster data-transfer speeds should continue to increase socket content per
device. We expect this trend to support long-term growth in the socket market, with
technology leaders such as WinWay well positioned to gain share through next-

generation solutions including HyperSocket.
Beyond its existing product portfolio, WinWay is developing next-generation
technologies including CPO electrical test sockets and liquid-cooled sockets for future
high TDP AI accelerators, reinforcing its position within the AI/HPC testing ecosystem.

WinWay's competitive advantages are its 1) technology leadership with coaxial socket
and HyperSocket, 2) proximity to the semiconductor testing value chain in Taiwan, and
3) historical relationship with key AI companies such as Nvidia and AMD. We believe

these advantages may allow WinWay to capture significant upside in the growing
semiconductor testing TAM driven by cloud AI proliferation, and gain share across its
competition such as Smiths Interconnect for AI and HPC applications.

Competition include:
 WinWay (6515.TW) : A major share gainer in AI/HPC sockets, with strong

coaxial sockets and is developing its next-generation HyperSocket platform. It is

also expanding into probe cards through its partnership with Technoprobe.

and memory sockets.

WinWay's margin is set to recover in 2027E/28E
Company GM hit a trough in Q226, at 38.3%. We view recent margin pressure as
largely temporary. GM has been impacted by MEMS probe sourcing through
Technoprobe (probe card GM now below 40% and dilutive to >50% coaxial socket GM)
and start-up costs associated with WinWay's rapid capacity expansion. During the early
part of 2025, WinWay's GM was high at 48.9% in H125, with more cobra probe cards

(60% GM) in mix with solid pogo pin and socket yield. With MEMS probe card now
accounting for more than 70% of probe card revenue and GM at around low to mid
30%, margin dilution from probe card business is unlikely to unravel for GM to return to
above 45% in the near term due to the MEMS partnership terms with Technoprobe.
However, we expect that as new facilities ramp and yields improve, profitability to
recover gradually from the Q226 trough. We see GM improving to 43.1% in 2027E and

44.7% in 2028E.

Appendix: What are semiconductor test sockets?
Semiconductor test socket are electro-mechanical interface that are used post chip
probing and chip packaging process to test the functionality of the chips. Sockets are
placed onto load boards on top of testers during the final test/system level test
processes. Handlers will place the chips onto the sockets, and pins inside the sockets will
enable the testers to read the signals from the device under test (DUT, i.e. the packaged

chips), with tester signals fan out  from beneath the PCB load board.
Figure 27: Socket illustration (with load
board underneath)
Source: UBS
As AI accelerators become larger and more complex, socket requirements continue to
increase. High-end sockets typically comprise a socket housing, alignment plate,
integrated thermal-control components and contact elements that connect the DUT to
the tester. Spring probes (pogo pins) are the most common choice for HPC sockets, with

high durability, individual probe replacement flexibility, and consistent contact force. FT
and SLT sockets account for more than 70% of the total socket market, with burn-in
sockets representing the remainder.

Despite a 120% YTD share-price increase, WinWay remains 43% below its April 2026
peak and trades at only 16x our 2028E P/E. We believe the market remains overly
focused on near-term GM pressure while underestimating the company's multi-year
growth opportunity. Our reverse DCF analysis indicates that the current market price
implies only a 19% long-term sales CAGR vs our estimate of 25%, alongside long-term
GM level of 32% vs our estimate of 45%.

We believe investors are not fully reflecting three key drivers:
1) CPU and TPU opportunities are becoming increasingly meaningful.
Beyond its core GPU socket franchise, WinWay is positioned to benefit from Nvidia's
Vera CPU, AMD's Venice CPU, and Google's TPU. These programmes should broaden
revenue sources and create an additional demand driver for high-end sockets and probe
cards.
2) Larger AI packages are driving higher socket content per device.

As AI accelerators adopt larger form factors, higher pin counts and greater power
consumption, socket complexity and ASPs continue to rise. We expect socket content
growth to outpace semiconductor unit growth, creating a structural tailwind for
WinWay.
3) Capacity ramp and margin recovery are not fully reflected.
Following substantial capacity expansion, we expect revenue growth of
105%/74%/71% in 2026E/27E/28E. At the same time, we forecast gross margin

recovery from 38.3% in Q226 to 43.1%/44.7% in 2027E/28E as utilization and yields
improve.
Our NT$8,900 price target is based on our valuation for WinWay at 30x average 2027E-
28E P/E, in-line with its historical average of 30x and underpinned by a 30% 2027-31E
earnings CAGR. Comparing with historical NTM peer average PE of roughly 25x
(currently trading at 39x NTM PE), we believe WinWay deserves to trade at a premium
given its leadership in AI/HPC sockets, expanding CPU and TPU exposure, and our long-

term earnings CAGR estimate.
WinWay is trading at NT$6,130 as of
6 Oct 2026

Company Description
Company Description
WinWay was founded in 2001 in Kaoshiung, Taiwan. It is a
semiconductor testing interface solution provider, with final test
sockets as its main business segment. The company has high-end
solutions for AI/HPC testing, providing final test/system-level test
and burn-in test sockets and probe cards through its own-made
probes and a partnership with Technoprobe.

Industry Outlook
New iterations of semiconductors, especially for AI / HPC
applications, are set to increase in both front-end wafer
fabrication advancements and back-end packaging complexities
with each iterations. With the industry now moves to utilize
"shift left", chip probing and its relevant supply chain will be key
beneficiaries of this trend which we believe will unlock multi-
year, secular growth trends.

solution provider, with final test sockets as its main business segment. The company has high-
end solutions for AI/HPC testing, providing final test/system-level test and burn-in test sockets

Valuation Method and Risk Statement

Risks for the semis industry:
- Geopolitical uncertainties: Geopolitical uncertainties including tariffs and more export
control could negatively impact semis cycle, and a downturn would lead to slower demand in
the overall market and moderating capacity expansion.
- Meaningful deceleration of new technology development: Slower demand and less

investment are expected If there is meaningful deceleration of new technology development
such as Cloud AI, autonomous driving, etc.
- Rising utility cost: Larger-than-expected utility cost increases would further pressure
profitability. Rising utility costs in Taiwan have negatively impacted margins for Taiwan tech
companies.
Company risks:
- Faster- or slower-than-expected CoWoS capacity expansion: This could result in WinWay
gaining/losing socket/probe card orders from major end customers, foundries, and OSATs.

- Competitors achieving technological advancement: Potential risk of competition (from
either local or foreign companies) achieving technological advancements could result in
WinWay losing market share from major end customers, foundries and OSATs.
- Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth: Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth could
lead to a ramp-up or a slowdown of equipment orders from major foundries and OSATs to
WinWay, affecting long-term revenue growth potential.

- Slowdown or delay of new technological advancement: A potential slowdown or delay of
the rollout for technological advancements for technologies such as SoIC, CoPoS, and CPO,
would limit future socket/probe card orders.
- Increase of raw material costs: Rising raw material costs may directly impact WinWay’s
margins for socket/probe card shipments.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

Document 201: 9月金融数据点评：好于预期的数据从哪来？

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月金融数据点评：好于预期的数据从哪来？.pdf
Report source: 国信证券
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

事项：
9 月份，新增社会融资规模 3.55万亿元，同比多增 192亿元；新增人民币贷款 1.08万亿元，同比少增 2100
亿元。
评论：
图1：金融数据分项一览
资料来源：Wind，国信证券经济研究所整理
结论：9 月金融数据总体好于市场预期，开年后持续走弱的局面有所缓和。但结构上，政府债券支撑社融
季末回升，非政府部门融资需求仍然较弱。社融新增 3.55万亿元、同比多增 192亿元；信贷新增 1.08 万
亿元，但同比仍少增 2100 亿元。企业中长贷是贷款端主要支撑，居民端暂时停止缩表；M1回落至 3.2%、
与 M2的剪刀差重新走阔至 4.0 个百分点；财政资金投放加快，9 月财政缺口预计冲高。值得注意的是，新
型政策性金融工具开始落地，9月投放规模或许显著超过部分测算，达 3000亿左右。
展望后续，10 月低基数下金融数据同比读数有望延续改善。去年 10月新增贷款 2200 亿元、新增社融 8178
亿元，基数偏低。四季度新型政策性金融工具 9 月启动首批投放后将持续发力，叠加近期的购房贷款贴息

政策，对企业中长期贷款和居民按揭能构成一定支撑。但在实体融资需求实质性回暖之前，贷款余额与社
融存量同比的下行趋势将会持续。
具体看，9 月数据：

### Page 2

社融季末回升，主要靠政府债支撑。9 月新增社融 3.55万亿元，同比多增 192 亿元；政府债券净融资 1.53
万亿元、同比多增 3438亿元，是同比转正的主要支撑；扣除政府债券的非政府融资同比少增 3246亿元，
显示实体融资需求仍然较弱。此外，当月新型政策性金融工具集中落地，交叉比对公开信息发现，其投放
规模或达 3000亿元左右（其中直接公布的约 1000亿元为民间投资），即非政府融资中亦含有政策驱动的
成分。
信贷季末回升超预期，居民端停止缩表。9 月新增人民币贷款 1.08 万亿，高于市场预期，同比少增 2100
亿元，贷款余额增速降至 4.8%、续创新低；企业中长贷是主要支撑，居民贷款净增为零，结束了连续两个
月的缩表。
总体来看，信用扩张中枢下移的趋势尚未扭转。政府债券融资放量叠加财政存款下降，财政资金季末投放
加快；受去年同期基数效应影响，M1 增速回落至 3.2%、与 M2 的剪刀差重新走阔。10 月低基数下同比读数
有望改善，随着政策效果逐步显现，四季度信贷有望边际修复。

解读：
 社融：政府债为主要支撑，非政府融资仍弱
9 月新增社融 3.55万亿元，较 8 月的 1.66 万亿元明显回升，同比多增 192 亿元，基本持平于过去五年同
期约 3.57万亿元的均值；月末社融存量同比增长 7.1%，较上月回落 0.1个百分点，继续创 2003年有数据
以来的新低。结构上，政府债券净融资 1.53万亿元，占新增社融的 43.1%，同比多增 3438亿元，贡献度
达 1791%，是社融同比转正的主要支撑。9 月是社融季节性回升月份，当月环比增加约 1.89万亿元，明显
高于过去五年同期平均的约 0.74万亿元。各科目对同比增量的贡献排序：政府债券（+3438亿元）、外币
贷款（+478亿元）、直接融资（-402亿元）、非标（-1102亿元）、人民币贷款（-3342 亿元）
1
。
图2：社融的季节性 图3：新增社融及结构
资料来源:Wind，国信证券经济研究所整理 资料来源:Wind，国信证券经济研究所整理
 信贷：季末回升超预期，居民端停止缩表
信贷季末回升，高于市场预期。9月新增人民币贷款 1.08 万亿元，高于市场普遍预期的约 9900亿元，但

低于过去五年同期约 1.86 万亿元的均值，同比少增 2100亿元；9 月末贷款余额同比增长 4.8%，较上月回
落 0.1个百分点，继续创有数据以来新低。
居民端暂时停止缩表。9 月居民部门贷款净增为零，结束 7-8 月连续两个月的净减少：短期贷款减少 900
亿元、中长期贷款增加 874 亿元，分别同比少增 2321亿元和 1626亿元；9 月末落地的房贷贴息政策对按
1
ABS和贷款核销两项尚未公布。

### Page 3

揭需求的提振效果尚待观察。
企业贷款同比多增，中长贷为主要支撑。企业部门贷款增加 1.29万亿元，同比多增 700 亿元：中长期贷
款增加 8900 亿元，同比仅少增 200亿元，是当月企业端信贷的最主要支撑；短期贷款增加 4600亿元，同
比少增 2500 亿元；票据融资减少 500亿元，季末未现票据冲量特征，一定程度上显示主管部门认为 9 月
企业贷款处于合理区间。非银金融机构贷款减少 1485 亿元。

 非标、直接融资与政府债券
非政府融资需求仍然较弱。扣除政府债券后，9 月非政府融资约 2.02万亿元，同比少增 3246亿元：社融
口径人民币贷款增加 1.27 万亿元，同比少增 3342亿元；直接融资增加 233亿元，同比少增 402亿元，主

要是企业债券净融资减少 425亿元、同比少增 561亿元，一定程度上与信贷投放形成“跷跷板”，可能主
要与城投化债有关；而境内股票同比依然维持多增（159亿元）；非标融资增加 2477亿元，同比少增 1102
亿元，其中委托贷款增加 802亿元、同比多增 519亿元，反映新型政策性金融工具投放加快，信托贷款减
少478亿元，未贴现银行承兑汇票增加2153亿元。前9个月债券与股票融资合计占社融增量的比重为49.5%，
仍高于贷款占比。

 4. 货币与存款：财政资金季末投放加快
M1增速回落，剪刀差重新走阔。9 月 M2 同比增长 7.2%，较上月回落 0.3个百分点，低于市场普遍预期的
7.45%，主要受信贷派生偏弱拖累；M1 同比增长 3.2%，受基数影响回落 0.9 个百分点，与 M2 的剪刀差重
新走阔至 4.0个百分点。
存款端，财政资金季末投放加快，非银存款流出。9 月新增人民币存款 1.77万亿元，同比少增 4400 亿元：
财政存款减少 4300亿元、同比多减 4100亿元，与政府债融资放量合计的单月财政赤字显著扩大，显示季
末财政支出大幅提速；居民存款增加 2.4万亿元、同比少增 5600 亿元；企业存款增加 1.31万亿元、同比
多增约 3900 亿元；非银存款减少 1.91万亿元、同比多减 8500 亿元，是存款端的主要拖累。

### Page 1

삼성바이오로직스 (207940/KS) 
3Q26 Pre: 파업과 환율 부담에도 컨센 부합 
2026-10-06

제약/바이오. 이선경 / seonkyoung.lee@sks.co.kr / 3773-9089

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 2,000,000 원(유지) 
현재주가: 1,354,000 원 
상승여력: 47.7%

Signal: 파업과 환율 부담에도 컨센 부합 전망  
Key: 5 공장 매출 본격화, 미국 공장 매출 기여에 따른 성장 기대   
Step: 단기 불확실성보다는 중장기적 성장 방향성에 주목

3Q26 Pre: 파업과 환율 부담에도 성장지속, 컨센 부합 전망   
26년 3분기 연결기준 매출액은 1조 3,741억원(+9.3%YoY, +4.0%QoQ), 영업이
익은 6,098억원(-3.7%YoY, +4.0%QoQ, OPM 44.4%)으로 컨센서스 매출액 1
조 3,450억원과 영업이익 6,063억원에 부합하는 실적을 전망한다.  
5월 파업 영향으로 약 20개 배치 생산분(약 1,500억원)에 대한 원가 손실이 인식되
고 3분기 평균 환율이 전분기 대비 약 5% 하락했음에도 불구하고, 3분기부터 매출로 
인식되는 5공장 PPQ 배치 및 미국 공장의 매출 기여로 지속적인 성장이 예상된다. 약 
1,500억원 규모의 생산 차질분은 고객사와 일정 조율을 통해 출하될 예정으로 이에 
따른 4분기 매출 성장을 기대해 볼 수 있다.   
신규 수주 회복과 모달리티 확장에 주목 
시장에서 우려했던 신규 수주는 관세 이슈로 고객사의 컨택이 감소하면서 올해 저조했
으나 4 월 Section 232 결과 발표 이후 논의가 회복 단계에 있어 연내 추가 신규 수주 
가능성은 열려 있다.  
항체 시장의 경쟁심화와 차세대 모달리티 확장에 대응하기 위해 결정한 폴리펩타이드
의 인수는 연내 마무리될 예정이다. 폴리펩타이드의 2028 년 목표 EBITDA 마진은 
25%로 동사 대비 낮은 상황이나, 펩타이드 기반 CDMO 시장 성장에 따른 마진 개선
도 가능하다. 현재 임단협, 환율, 미국 공장 및 폴리펩타이드 인수에 따른 회계 영향 등 
불확실성이 큰 상황이나, 수주 회복과 5 공장 매출 인식 본격화, 미국 공장 및 멀티 모
달리티 확장 등 중장기 성장 방향성에 주목할 필요가 있다.

### Page 1

TAKING STOCK

The Reserve Bank of India (RBI) has adopted a 
multi-layered defence strategy to support the 
rupee this year.

This has entailed a combination of outright 
intervention in spot/ forwards, regulatory 
action, liquidity steps, administrative measures 
and moves to curb import -related dollar 
demand.

The rupee has appreciated by 1.8% against the 
dollar in the past five days since recent 
measures took effect, paring YTD depreciation 
to 5.5% . High oil prices, weakness in capital 
inflows, a firm dollar , tariffs/ geopolitical 
worries, energy , and risk -off sentiments have 
kept the INR under pressure since last year.

India: Degrees of rupee defence

16 October 2026 
Radhika Rao, Senior Economist 
radhikarao@dbs.com

### Page 2

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            2

ECHOES OF 2013 WITH FEW 
DIFFERENCES

Comparisons with 2013 are apt, though 
the backdrop differs.  As for the 
catalysts, markets reacted to the US 
Fed’s taper announcement in 2013, 
which pushed US yields higher  and led 
global investors to reduce exposure to 
vulnerable EM economies. As a result, 
capital inflows weakened, exposing 
funding mismatch es for countries, 
including India, that were running wide 
current account deficits.

In 2026, markets face the spectre of 
elevated oil prices, geopolitical 
uncertainty, global competition for 
capital, a firm dollar ( DXY appreciated 
+3.8% YTD) , and US inflation/ deficits 
which have settled at levels much higher 
than pre -pandemic years. This has 
pushed up UST yields, and the US Fed 
kicked off its rate-hike cycle in Sep26.

Against this backdrop, the RBI is 
pursuing the same objective , i.e., to 
ensure rupee stability, but the nature of 
the battle differs. The defence strategy 
has shifted from ‘crisis management’ 
mode in 2013 , due to weaker domestic 
macroeconomic balances (a wide current 
account gap, high inflation, a lower 
reserve buffer, and wide fiscal deficits) , 
to a ‘terms-of-trade shock and impact of 
a firm dollar’ in 2026.

Flashpoints are  nonetheless similar, 
with reliance on portfolio financing , 
leaving the external account vulnerable 
to shifts in global risk sentiment. 
Portfolio outflows have emerged as the 
primary transmission channel through 
which external shocks have translated 
into INR depreciation pressure , 
compounded by high energy prices and a 
firm dollar.

FX PLAYBOOK

While the response framework is similar 
between 2013 and this year, the broad 
sequence and nature of macroprudential 
measures differ. This year’s multi-
layered rupee defence toolkit has 
included:

-Direct FX defence  by way of 
intervention in the spot and forwards 
market. As of Aug26, the net forward 
dollar liabilities ballooned to $200bn, 
rising by ~$63bn vs . the month before . 
Recent sell/buy FX swaps are expected 
to lower this stock in the coming months.

-Attracting dollar inflows  by way of 
special swap facilities for FCNR deposits, 
and offshore borrowings , apart from 
liberalising taxes around debt inflows,

### Page 3

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            3

amongst others. We discussed this here, 
here, here and here.

Taking a proactive stance, a portion of 
existing FX reserves could be earmarked 
against forward liabilities (from maturing 
FCNR deposits) , helping to mitigate 
concerns that deposit maturities or debt 
repayments could trigger a sharp
increase in dollar ⁠demand down the line.

-Domestic liquidity management. Bonds, 
to some degree, are paying the price for 
the authorities' efforts to defend the 
rupee against the additional headwind of 
weak global conditions.  INR liquidity 
surged on the back of the swap windows, 
prompting the RBI to undertake open 
market operations, VRRR auctions, sell -
buy swaps, among others. While these 
have been effective in absorbing 
balances, bond yields have drifted higher. 
10Y yields are at three-year highs and are 
expected to climb further  (DBSf: 7.40 -
7.50% by year-end).

-Moderate FX demand and speculation . 
Earlier in the year, the RBI had 
introduced change s to the banks’ net 
open position framework, and thereafter 
partially rolled back a few curbs. 
Prudential measures on FX exposures 
and hedging -related measures were 
announced in Oct26, which include d the 
introduction of a daily cash reserve with 
the RBI equal to 20% of the rupee 
equivalent of the notional value for INR -
linked derivative contracts exceeding 
$2mn used to hedge current account 
transactions, referred to as the Foreign 
Exchange Risk Reserve (FERR) . 
Subsequent clarifications provided 
operational details on where the FERR 
would be maintained and that it would be 
subject to audits.

Since 12 Oct, the RBI has been providing 
dollars via designated banks to the three 
state-owned oil -marketing companies 
(Indian Oil, BPCL, and HPCL) to meet the 
their daily dollar requirements, thereby 
diverting related spot-market demand.

On hedging-related matters, the limit for 
undertaking foreign exchange derivative 
transactions without proving an 
underlying commercial exposure was 
cut to $5mn from $100mn across all ADs 
and exchange -traded currency 
derivatives. Restrictions on Rebooking , 
i.e., a uthorized dealers are prohibited 
from allowing customers to rebook 
cancelled rupee -linked derivative 
contracts (though rollovers upon 
maturity remain permitted). Lastly, 
banks must obtain written confirmation 
from users that the same underlying 
exposure has not been hedged with 
another dealer.  Data from CCI , cited by

### Page 4

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            4

Bloomberg, pointed to importers’ 
forward dollar purchases rising 44% on 
the year to a monthly average of $64bn.

-Current account management . Efforts 
to contain structural FX outflows by way 
of higher duties and taxes on gold 
imports discourage non -essential 
purchases and production incentives to 
improve export competitiveness.

IMPACT

To assess whether the playbook has 
worked, we observe:

a) Rupee has enjoyed a brief respite. 
Despite oil prices at $105/bbl at the time 
of writing and elevated global yields, 
USDINR fell from near 96.80 to wards 
95.00, owing to measures announced in 
Oct26 following the central bank’s 25bp 
hike. Beyond temporary relief, global 
catalysts are likely to be back to the fore 
and weigh on the currency (see chart);

b) Sharp inflows under the swap 
facilities pushed the foreign reserves 
stock to a record high, providing 
policymakers sufficient buffer to 
navigate future shocks from a position of 
strength. A strong interventionist 
response has since led to a softening in 
the overall stock (see chart).

c) Macroprudential measures have led to 
distortions in other parts of the market . 
For instance, strong swap -related 
inflows had inorganically pushed 
onshore INR liquidity up sharply, 
necessitating absorption measures. 
Similarly, NOP restrictions and the more 
recent tighter requirements on hedging 
activity and counterparty transactions  
had some after -effects. Forward points 
have surged this week , with financial 
institutions pointing to relative illiquidity 
in the currency forwards market  on 
volumes and activity basis, and fewer 
avenues to deal with dollar supply.

### Page 5

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            5

d) Swap-related windows are a timely 
support for the country’s balance of 
payments position. From expectations of 
a third consecutive year of BOP deficit in 
FY27, the math has now swung to a 
potential surplus, even as current 
account worries remain. With energy 
prices settling north of $100/bbl, 
keeping the monthly trade deficit near 
$30bn, our FY27 current account deficit 
forecast of 1.1% of GDP faces  upside 
risks of around 0.3-0.5% of GDP. We note 
the s harp jump in ECB outflows in S ep, 
though see that as a temporary overshot 
of leverage-FCNR activity.

WHAT LIES AHEAD

Authorities are likely not done yet and 
remain ready to take further action, as 
required. RBI Governor Malhotra 
reiterated the authorities’ serious intent 
of stabilising the currency in a recent 
interview (Moneycontrol, 13 Oct), 
emphasising that “The RBI is determined 
to do whatever is necessary to ensure 
orderly movement in the foreign 
exchange market,”. He also highlighted 
that recent steps were not tantamount to 
capital control measures that were 
aimed at ensuring that forex markets 
serve genuine hedging needs rather than 
speculative bets.  Market participants 
opine that FX volumes and interest are 
likely to be subdued in a few pockets on 
the back of the recent measures, 
consequently stabilizing the rupee.

Unlike in 2013, when policy tightening 
was a part of the initial response, the 
2026 strategy has prioritised targeted 
FX and regulatory measures.  The 
sequencing suggests a deliberate effort 
to distinguish actions aimed at 
preserving currency stability from those 
intended to achieve domestic monetary 
objectives, with rates remaining a 
potential, though not immediate, tool in 
the defence framework.

However, global developments have 
altered that dynamic . Rising US yields 
and a gradual increase in the US Fed 
Funds rate (DBS baseline: a total of 75bp 
hikes) have compressed IN -US spreads. 
The recent sell -off in INR benchmark

### Page 6

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            6

bonds has effectively widened Gsec -
UST spreads across maturities, in 
contrast to the compression witnessed in 
regional high-yielders.

The latest announcements may not 
fully quell speculation over further 
policy action.

Upcoming measures might include -

-Markets anticipate relief on the long -
term capital gains tax structure on 
equities to draw in longer-duration 
inflows. This measure will boost inflows 
if it coincides with other factors that 
make domestic equities attractive for 
thematic investing , as global risk -free 
rates moderate.

-Street expectations also include steps 
to boost inward dollar repatriation by 
exporters to boost domestic dollar 
supply.

-Wider policy rate corridor akin to 2013.

-Policy rate response. While this might 
not be a panacea, it is a likely necessary 
condition. Raising the neutral rate might 
have mixed success in attracting capital 
market inflows ; though rate-sensitive 
flows are likely to get a fillip as investors 
seek attractive rate differentials in the 
region.

An adequate real rate cushion would 
also align with the evolving domestic 
inflation realities, which are expected to 
accelerate to 6% in the Oct-Dec quarter 
and remain above 5% well into 2027.

Given fluid global developments, we 
outline three scenarios on the rate path 
ahead. Our baseline assumption  is for 
75bp hikes in this cycle  (including Oct), 
spread between the Dece mber and 
February 2027 meetings. We don’t rule 
out an intermeeting rate hike before the 
December meeting, contingent on 
rupee movements.

Under the second scenario , s hould 
depreciation pressures on the rupee 
return, the 50bp hike might be brought 
forward to December , with another in 
February, taking cumulative hikes in this 
cycle to 100bp. The t hird scenario  is a 
more aggressive 150bp worth of hikes in 
this cycle, with 50bp front -loaded in 
December, and the rest spilling into 2027. 
Factors that  will determine whether 
these scenarios materialise are the 
resumption and scale of rupee 
depreciation, oil price direction, US Fed 
Funds rate increases (DBSf: total 75bps) 
and other risk-off events.

Path ahead

Despite these measures, we see limited 
room for sustained INR appreciation on 
grounds of manufacturing/ trade 
competitiveness. A challenging flows 
portfolio flows outlook leaves the d oor 
open to further gradual rupee 
depreciation subject to global 
developments, with high sensitivity to oil 
price movements and risk sentiments . A 
strong rate response will help introduce  
favourable rate differentials and reduce 
currency volatility.

Document 442: What’s the Renminbi Got to Do With it_ (2026-10-11)

Source PDF: all report pdf/What’s the Renminbi Got to Do With it_ (2026-10-11).pdf
Report source: CFR (Follow the Money)
Date: 2026-10-11
Pages: 17
Status: ok

### Page 1

1
EXPERT TAKE FOLLOW THE MONEY
What’s the Renminbi Got to Do With it?
A closer look at the impact of China’s currency on China’s export growth.
Sean Yong/Reuters
UPDATED
October 11, 2026 12:00 a.m.
Articles Follow the Money Share
Log In
Regions Topics Research Experts Events The Future of American Strategy

### Page 2

2
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
There is an argument—made in the recent Geneva
Report and by various former IMF officials—that
China’s current export surge is the simple
consequence of the housing downturn and the
resulting weakness in Chinese internal demand.*
The weakness in the RMB in this story is entirely
incidental to the increase in exports, which is, more or
less, a mechanical response to weak internal demand.

Cannot sell excavators at home? Export them.
Built more modern car capacity than your internal
market supports? Export that too…
The fall in the nominal RMB over the course of 2022
and the first half of 2023 in nominal and read terms
was simply a function of domestic macroeconomic
weakness, and a growing rate differential. It wasn’t an
active policy choice by China’s central bank.

### Page 3

3
Now, some might note that China’s currency doesn’t
always move with rate differentials. The RMB didn’t
consistently get pulled up when Chinese rates were
higher than U.S. rates (2002-2020 generally
speaking), and while it did fall when rate differentials
moved against the RMB in 2022 and 2023, it then
stabilized and has even appreciated modestly since
the middle of last year. That appreciation hasn’t been
big enough to correct the deep undervaluation of the

RMB, but it came at a time when the PBOC policy
rate (the reverse repo rate) was well below the U.S.
policy rate.
China doesn’t have an open financial account. As a
result, equilibrium in the foreign exchange market is
thus driven far more by exporter conversion and
current account flows than by rate differentials and
financial flows.** That, together with China’s ongoing
management of the exchange rate and a trade surplus
that can generate significant inflows, means that the

### Page 4

4
value of the RMB is much more of a policy variable
under the independent control of the Chinese
authorities than a simple byproduct of monetary and
fiscal policies.
The modern IMF is reluctant to accept this
conclusion. Its surveillance has generally ignored
China’s currency management (even if that
management is relatively obvious; see the data on FX
settlement) and its recent comprehensive assessment
of China’s economy completely ignored the balance of

payments and exchange rate management.
But it is pretty obvious to the market, even if the
PBOC, rather absurdly, insists that “market forces
play a decisive role in determining the RMB
exchange rate.”
I doubt the PBOC can win this argument. I agree
with Mark Sobel:
“The renminbi is still heavily managed and the
authorities have acted to slow appreciation. The paper
states that the PBoC phased out ‘regular’ foreign
exchange intervention in 2017. But it has many

means at its disposal to achieve the same effect
through extensive capital controls, administrative
guidance, signalling at the fix and deploying state-
owned commercial banks. These means are heavily
used. Thus, the word ‘regular’ must be taken with a
pinch of salt. The comparison to Japan, which

### Page 5

5
intervenes occasionally, is spurious – the yen largely
floats freely in integrated global markets. The same
cannot be said for the renminbi.”
In reality, the PBOC’s daily fix now plays the decisive
role in the currency’s path, and the market is often a
bet on how the PBOC’s management of the yuan will
evolve.
That is clear from the lack of volatility in the yuan’s
rate against the dollar. The yuan’s move down against
the dollar in 2022 was a controlled 10 percent move,

and the appreciation over the last 16 months has been
even more controlled crawl.
And the market is clearly massaged with substantial
help from the state banks. In fact that the pattern of
changes in the monthly FX settlement data looks
exactly like the pattern of intervention of a central
bank managing its currency around a tight band (the

### Page 6

6
state banks in practice buy FX to keep the CNY from
testing the strong side of the band, so there is an
asymmetry in how the CNY is managed)
None of this would matter much if China’s economy
was small. But that obviously isn’t the case. Nor would
this matter much if China’s exports didn’t respond to
changes in the RMB’s real (and nominal) value. But
that is also not the case.
There is abundant evidence that China’s exports do
respond to moves in the currency. Periods of RMB

weakness have consistently been associated with
periods of especially rapid Chinese export growth.

### Page 7

7
Chancellor Merz and President Macron got this
right, even if macroeconomic issues haven’t yet
figured prominently in the EU’s dialogue with
China.*** As they note in their open letter to the
European Commission:
“An appreciation of undervalued currencies towards a value
more in line with fundamentals would help support the
rebalancing of trade accounts with the EU in the short and
medium term … European policy discussions focused on
forging a common understanding of these exchange rate issues

and their interaction with global imbalances would be useful.”
The best evidence that exports responded to changes
in the currency, and not simply to changes in the path
of China’s internal demand, is the acceleration in
China’s export growth after the RMB’s depreciation
over the course of 2022.
This is the killer chart.

### Page 8

8
Now, you might ask: what of the acceleration in
export growth in 2020 and 2021? That was pretty
clearly tied to the global increase in goods demand
that came with the pandemic and work from home
requirements. It was expected to fade when the world
economy normalized. But it didn’t. By 2023 Chinese
export growth again topped global trade growth.
The general expectation that the rise in China’s goods
surplus during Covid would prove to be “transitory”

### Page 9

9
heavily influenced the IMF’s work at the time: see the
IMF’s external sector reports of 2023 and 2024.
I would argue that neglecting the exchange rate was
the key reason the IMF’s kept forecasting, incorrectly,
that China’s current account surplus would fall (See
this blog with Shahin Vallee). The IMF certainly
didn’t warn that China’s property downturn and the
RMB’s real depreciation risked a second China shock
in its 2022, 2023, or 2024 surveillance reports.

Concretely, the weaker RMB appears to have
contributed to a very large outperformance of China’s
exports relative to global trade. See the following
chart
That incidentally also happened after the RMB
followed the dollar down in 2003.
In both periods, there were significant structural
shifts. The WTO accession in 2002, and the success

### Page 10

10
of Chinese industrial policies in the “green” industrial
sectors and autos in 2022. In both cses exports were
turbocharged when structural shifts that should have
resulted in a stronger currency were instead amplified
by currency weakness.
No matter. Paul Krugman has noted that a country
that is converging technologically with its trade
partners should see a trend appreciation in its
currency. China got the technological convergence,
but not the real appreciation—at least not until this

year. And the move since the middle of 2025 has
corrected the depreciation of the RMB that occurred
when the RMB followed the dollar down on
liberation day, but not the structural overvaluation.
Now, there is an argument that exchange rate
adjustment is too hot an issue to make it a priority.
The PBOC isn’t interested it seems.
And some believe that in the interest of comity and
good relations with China, the IMF should mirror
the PBOC’s views. After all a stronger RMB would

slow China’s exports and thus slow China’s economy
at a time when China needs export growth to escape
deflation and generate employment. Or so the
argument goes. Concerns about “external balance“
and the negative global spillovers from China’s rising
goods surplus should be ignored and the Fund’s sole
focus should be on policies that lead to internal

### Page 11

11
balance. That provides one rationale for the Fund’s
ongoing decision to essentially ignore the contentious
issue of the PBOC’s ongoing exchange rate
management.
That argument, however, assumes that China’s
economy is small and thus China’s external surplus
can safely be ignored because it isn’t a drag on the
growth of the IMF’s other members.
That clearly isn’t true.
And it isn’t clear that China is more interested in
having a negotiation over its level of basic pension

benefits or the size of its fiscal deficit (where the
IMF continues to make timid recommendations that
would not move the needle—and certainly wouldn’t
meaningfully reduce China’s external surplus) than a
discussion over its currency practices.****
Back in the world of fixed but adjustable exchange
rates, there was an expectation (not always met to be
sure) that surplus countries would allow their
currencies to strengthen to bring their economies

back into external balance, and that other policy tools
would be used to ensure internal balance.
I would argue that China’s exchange rate (against
both the dollar and the basket) has many similarities
to the old world of fixed but adjustable currencies.
True, it moves on a controlled path when it moves

### Page 12

12
between different levels rather than jumping up and
down. But the PBOC does frequently target a level,
and that targeted level does change over time.
I would further argue that steps like the recent tariff
rate quota that will limit China’s exports of hybrid
cars to Europe to around 350,000 units next year (up
from 2025, but well below the 800,000 units of
2026) aren’t going to address the underlying
imbalance on their own. They can provide a bit of a

buffer for specific sectors.
But that buffer, while necessary, won’t address the
broad-based erosion of manufacturing capacity
outside China. This erosion will persist as long as an
undervalued RMB sends the entire world a signal to
use China as its production base.
The new system of managed trade that is emerging in
autos would need to be progressively expanded to an
ever-increasing set of sectors if protection is used as a
substitute for both domestic reform in China and

exchange rate adjustment.
That is now the direction of travel, but it doesn’t
address the underlying systemic issues.
Incidentally, those who believe the path of internal
demand is critical to rebalancing and that exchange
rate moves are secondary need to explain how an
acceleration in domestic demand would, on its own,

### Page 13

13
lead China’s external accounts to adjust. One
important, stylized fact about China is that domestic
demand growth is no longer feeding over to import
growth (ex-gold, that is).
Bottom line: China, which needs a 1.5 percentage
point contribution from net exports every year, is on a
collision course with its trading partners without a
currency correction that provides the basis for more
balanced trade even in the absence of trade
restrictions.
And there isn’t a realistic path towards balance that

doesn’t involve a stronger RMB; there aren’t, as Paul
Krugman once put it, immaculate transfers.
* That downturn shows up in domestic deflationary
pressure, and weak job growth/high rates of (at least
youth) unemployment. China, in other words, isn’t in
“internal balance.” I really liked the report from

### Page 14

14
Kamakshya Trivedi and Hui Shan of Goldman Sachs
arguing China needs two tools to hit two targets
(internal and external balance).
** To be sure, rate differentials matter for exporter
conversion—but so does the expected movement of
the RMB. The market understands this better than
the Fund. The Chen and Suwanapruti (Goldman
Sachs) note uses a framework similar to mine to
describe the CNY’s moves in Q3:
“In our view, sizeable FX inflows have continued to support

RMB appreciation despite unfavorable interest rate
differentials, as strong export receipts and increased exporter
FX conversions have outweighed portfolio outflows. Our
estimated FX conversion ratio rose to 63% in August,
compared with around 45% in normal years, likely reflecting a
shift in market expectations from RMB depreciation to
appreciation.”
*** Š ef č ovi č  has jurisdiction over trade policy
(narrowly defined), but not broader macroeconomic

policies. Europe’s institutional architecture for
exchange rate coordination is not well-developed.
**** The current account surplus is no longer masked
to the same extent as in 2023 and 2024, as SAFE has
fiddled with its BoP methodology once again (per the
Federal Reserve) and the BoP goods surplus is no
longer significantly below the customs goods surplus.
Of course, there are still problems in the data—the
deficit in the investment income account most

熱門議題 / 投資關鍵
《商品貨幣受制於強勢美元，人民幣溫和升值》
美元在9月FOMC會議再升一碼之後，美聯儲獨立
性有所增強，加上市場對於美國升息預計至明年
中仍有三碼之多，美元再次走強，DXY站上100。
市場對於做多澳幣有所遲疑，市場頭寸再次轉向
空頭主導，但我們認爲包括金和銅在內的大宗商
品價格的反彈令貿易條件的改善，澳幣在0.71以
下已經有了買入價值。人民幣進入年尾出口商有
結匯需求，惟留意中美利差擴大等因素制約，預
計4Q26人民幣偏向升值但空間溫和。

《投資關鍵-澳洲貨幣政策取決地緣與油價變化》
澳聯儲主席布洛克在 2026年9月22日的最新講話中明
確表示，4.5%的失業率目前“仍然偏緊”，失業率需
要升至4.5%~5.0%之間，才能有效為勞動力市場降溫
並緩解通膨壓力；然隨後公布的 8月失業率由 4.5%升
至4.7%，已落在央行目標區間。不過澳洲8月份CPI數
據，年增由3.5%升至4.0%為澳央9月再度升息的主因 ，
主要受到能源價格漲所致；然整體結構未見失控風險 ，
核心CPI年增率持平於3.6%，扣除扣居住與交通CPI年
增由2.2%降至2.1%，因此12月份會期升息與否取決
於油價能否回落 ， 如 地 緣 趨 緩 配 合 失 業 率 維 持 在
4.5~5.0%區間，澳央12月有暫停升息空間。

另外，觀察CFTC投機頭寸顯示，澳幣投機空頭已經大
量累積，做空倉位正在主導市場 ，短期認爲澳幣應在
0.7~0.71的目標價盤整，更多上行空間應來自於美元
降息預期的重新開啓 ，或更多在明年中期左右 。我們
認爲澳幣的看法仍然中性略為正向，而近期跌破0.7關
卡的價位下，有些許布局空間。
• 我們認爲澳幣短線中性偏正向，
在澳幣短期有所回撤 0.7以下價
位下，可適時加碼澳幣部位。

紐西蘭景氣修復逐步延續，中期復甦動能仍在
《投資關鍵-美元強勢壓抑紐元表現 ，惟中期復甦動能
仍在》
9月份紐元表現主要受外部因素主導 。儘管紐西蘭經濟
數據顯示改善跡象，但油價上升及美國財政赤字憂慮推
動美國長天期公債殖利率升至逾二十年高位，帶動美元
走強及市場重新評估美國利率前景 ，令NZD/USD由月
初接近0.59水平回落至0.56附近，紐元與其他高貝他貨
幣同步受壓。
經濟基本面方面 ，紐西蘭第二季 GDP季增0.2%、年增
2.6%，優於央行預期 ，顯示經濟持續復甦 。企業投資

及AI相關無形資產支出保持穩健，惟家庭消費及零售活
動仍然偏弱，反映內需復甦步伐相對溫和。貨幣政策方
面，市場預期RBNZ將於10月維持官方現金利率不變，
以評估既有緊縮政策的影響；惟若經濟活動及通膨壓力
持續展現韌性，年底前不排除進一步收緊政策的可能。
在主要已開發國家央行政策路徑逐漸分化的背景下，紐
西蘭相對較高的利率水準仍可為紐元提供一定支持，但
短期市場焦點預料仍集中於美國利率及美元走勢。
展望未來一至兩季，我們認為紐元仍有望受惠於國內經
濟成長改善及投資活動回暖，逐步擺脫近期低位。然而，
在美國利率維持較高水準及美元強勢延續的環境下，紐
元上行空間可能受到一定抑制 。因此，我們雖仍預期
NZD/USD 可自目前水準溫和回升，但將第四季目標價
由0.60下調至0.59，以反映美元強勢環境可能維持較長
時間。

通膨數據回落，6月降息的可能性加大重點摘要 《投資關鍵-政策觀望與美元強勢夾擊 ，加幣延續弱
勢格局》
中東局勢未見緩和，油價及通膨壓力維持高檔，促使
聯準會於9月16日升息25個基點，市場焦點亦由降息
預期轉向利差優勢及避險需求，導致非美貨幣承壓。
在市場風險情緒急轉直下，加幣自 USD/CAD 1.385
附近持續走弱，並一度來到 1.42以上。
加拿大央行料維持審慎觀望立場，市場對其轉向鷹派
的預期仍缺乏數據支持。儘管油價上升可能推升整體
通膨，但剔除能源因素後，加拿大經濟基本面尚未出
現足以支持進一步收緊政策的明顯改善。加上近期市
場已大幅回補加元空頭部位，在下一份就業報告公布
前，預料難有促使加拿大央行改變政策基調的催化劑，
因此貨幣政策前景仍偏向中性。
另一方面，高油價有助提升加拿大能源出口收入，對
加幣形成一定支撐；惟中東地緣壓力和美加貿易不確
定性猶存，料將拖慢加拿大經濟及就業復甦步伐。綜
合上述因素，我們預期加幣短期走勢仍偏疲弱，故將
4Q26美元兌加幣目標價由1.37上調至1.39。
